\displaystyle II^{\prime}_{2}=O\Big(\varepsilon^{-2}\int_{B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}}(0)}\varepsilon^{2}|x|^{2}dx\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{\frac{16}{5}})=o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{3}

and

\displaystyle II^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))+w_{i,\varepsilon}^{I}+(1-\chi_{t})(W^{**}_{i,\varepsilon}-W^{*}_{i,\varepsilon})}-1\Bigg\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))}-1\Bigg\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O\Bigg(\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(0)\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}(0)}}\frac{\varepsilon\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{|x|^{2}}dx\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))}-1\Bigg\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p^{*}_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O\Bigg(\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(0)\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}(0)}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{|x|^{3}}dx\Bigg)\varepsilon^{2}+O\Bigg(\varepsilon^{2}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(0)\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}(0)}}\frac{\varepsilon\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{|x|^{2}}dx\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))}-1\Big\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p^{*}_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O\Bigg(\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(0)\backslash B_{\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{5}}(0)}}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}{|x|^{3}}dx\Bigg)\varepsilon^{2}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\Bigg(e^{I_{i}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}\frac{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))}-1}{|x-p_{t}^{*}|^{4}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Bigg).

Notice that in the third equation, the first error is due to Hypothesis (

H
2

) and the difference between

\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))}-1\Bigg\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}

and

\Bigg\{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{t,\varepsilon})-{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon}))w_{i,\varepsilon}^{I}+(1-\chi_{t})(W^{**}_{i,\varepsilon}-W^{*}_{i,\varepsilon})}-1\Bigg\}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p^{*}_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}.

Notice the number

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\hat{I}}\frac{m^{*}-2}{N}\Big(D_{i,t}+\sum_{s\neq t}{\frac{e^{H_{i,s}(p^{*})}}{e^{H_{i,t}(p^{*})}}}D_{i,s}\Big)

(3.21)

is finite
due to
the assumption

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{N}{e^{H_{i,t}(p^{*})}}L_{i,t}=0
.
Here,

D_{i,s}:=\Bigg[\int_{\Omega_{t}}\frac{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{s}^{*})}e^{8\pi({G^{*}}(x;p_{s}^{*})-{G^{*}}(p_{s}^{*};p_{s}^{*}))}-1}{|x-p_{s}^{*}|^{4}}dx-\int_{\Omega_{t}^{c}}\frac{dx}{|x-p_{s}^{*}|^{4}}\Bigg]e^{I_{i}}

and

L_{i,t}:=\Bigg|\frac{\nabla h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}{h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}+8\pi\nabla_{1}{G^{*}}(p_{t}^{*};p_{t}^{*})\Bigg|^{2}e^{I_{i}}+\Bigg(\frac{\Delta h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}{h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}+8N\pi\Bigg)e^{I_{i}}.

Together, they prove the result.

\Box

3.5
Proof of Theorem
1.1

Now we prove Theorem
1.1
.
We only verify the Case
(1)
of Theorem
1.1
since Cases
(2)
and
(3)
are similar.
To do this, we will

•

Find a genuine solution to Problem (
1.1
);

•

Verify Hypotheses (

H
1

), (

H
2

) and (

H
3

);

•

Verify (

⁢
S

E
1

), (

⁢
S

E
2

) and (

⁢
S

E
3

).

By Lemma
3.2
and Proposition
3.3
, for
j=1,2
and
t=1,\cdots,N
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\Bigg(-\Delta(U_{\varepsilon}^{i}+w_{\varepsilon}^{i})-\rho_{i,\varepsilon}\Bigg(\frac{h_{i}(x)e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{i,\varepsilon}}}{\int_{\Omega}h_{i}(x)e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{i,\varepsilon}}}-1\Bigg)\Bigg)Z_{j,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\Bigg(-\Delta\Big(U_{\varepsilon}^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}w^{I}_{j,\varepsilon}\Big)-\rho_{i,\varepsilon}\Bigg(\frac{h_{i}(x)e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w^{i}_{i,\varepsilon}}}{\int_{\Omega}h_{i}(x)e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w^{I}_{i,\varepsilon}}}-1\Bigg)\Bigg)Z_{j,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}+R_{j,t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\partial_{x_{j}}\ln h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon})+2\pi m_{i}^{*}\partial_{x_{j}}{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon})\Big]\rho_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{t}+\varepsilon O_{m^{*}}.

By the non-degeneracy of the critical point
(p_{1}^{*},\cdots,p_{N}^{*})
to
\sum_{t=1}^{N}\Big\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\ln h_{i}(x_{t})+2\pi m_{i}^{*}{G^{*}}(x;p_{t}^{*})\Big]\rho_{i}^{*}\Big\}

such that
p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}}\to p_{t}^{*}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
and for any
t=1,\cdots,N

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\nabla\ln h_{i}(p_{t,\varepsilon})+2\pi m_{i}^{*}\nabla{G^{*}}(p_{t,\varepsilon};p_{t,\varepsilon})\Big)=O_{m^{*}}.

(3.22)

Therefore,
(p_{1,\varepsilon},\cdots,p_{N,\varepsilon})
satisfies Hypothesis (

H
2

).

By a similar idea, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}

\displaystyle\Bigg[-\Delta(U_{\varepsilon}^{i}+w_{\varepsilon}^{i})-\rho_{i,\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{h_{i}e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{\varepsilon}^{i}}}{\int_{\Omega}h_{i}e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{\varepsilon}^{i}}}-1\Big)\Bigg]Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\Bigg[-\Delta(U_{\varepsilon}^{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}w_{j,\varepsilon}^{I})-\rho_{i,\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{h_{i}e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{i,\varepsilon}^{I}}}{\int_{\Omega}h_{i}e^{U_{i,\varepsilon}+w_{i,\varepsilon}^{I}}}-1\Big)\Bigg]Z_{3,i,\varepsilon}^{*}+R_{3,t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{m_{i}^{*}-2}{N}\Big(D_{i,t}+\sum_{s\neq t}{\frac{e^{H_{i,s}(p^{*})}}{e^{H_{i,t}(p^{*})}}}D_{i,s}+o_{\delta}(1)\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{m^{*}_{i}-1}+2\pi\Lambda_{I,N}(\rho_{\varepsilon})\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{m^{*}-1}.

with

\displaystyle D_{i,s}:=\Bigg[\int_{\Omega_{t}}\frac{\frac{h_{i}(x)}{h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}e^{2\pi m_{i}^{*}({G^{*}}(x;p_{t}^{*})-{G^{*}}(p_{t}^{*};p_{t}^{*}))}-1}{|x-p_{t}^{*}|^{m_{i}^{*}}}dx-\int_{\Omega_{t}^{c}}\frac{dx}{|x-p_{t}^{*}|^{m_{i}^{*}}}\Bigg]e^{I_{i}}

for
s=1,\cdots,N
.
Due to the geometry of the hypersurface

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}=\{\rho=(\rho_{1},\cdots,\rho_{N})\in(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{N}|\Lambda_{I,N}(\rho)=0\},

we can find a sequence
\rho_{\varepsilon}=(\rho_{1,\varepsilon},\cdots,\rho_{n,\varepsilon})
such that

•

\rho_{i,\varepsilon}\to\rho_{i}^{*}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
;

•

\frac{\rho_{j,\varepsilon}-\rho_{j}^{*}}{\rho_{i,\varepsilon}-\rho_{i}^{*}}\sim 1
.

The existence of such a sequence is due to Sard’s theorem
[
21
]
.
Therefore, we can find a sequence
(\varepsilon_{1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{N})
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda(\rho_{\varepsilon})=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{m_{i}^{*}-2}{2\pi N}\Big(D_{i,t}+\sum_{s\neq t}{\frac{e^{H_{i,s}(p^{*})}}{e^{H_{i,t}(p^{*})}}}D_{i,s}+o_{\delta}(1)\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{m^{*}_{i}-2}.

(3.23)

This implies that Hypothesis (

H
2

) is satisfied.

Now we are in the position to verify Hypothesis (

H
3

).
The idea to verify Hypothesis (

H
3

) is to proceed the blow-up analysis for the constructed solution
(U_{1,\varepsilon}+w_{1,\varepsilon},\cdots,U_{n,\varepsilon}+w_{n,\varepsilon})
. Denote for any
t=1,\cdots,N
, choose

p_{t,\varepsilon}
to be the blow-up point. By the construction of the solution
(U_{1,\varepsilon}+w_{1,\varepsilon},\cdots,U_{n,\varepsilon}+w_{n,\varepsilon})
, we find that

\displaystyle M_{t,\varepsilon}^{i}:=U_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon})+w_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon})=U_{i}(0)+\ln\frac{1}{2\varepsilon_{t}}+O(|w^{0}_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})|)=U_{i}(0)+\ln\frac{1}{2\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}}.

Here,
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}
, the scale in the view point of blow up.
Then, we get

\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}=\varepsilon_{t}e^{O(|w^{0}_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})|)}
.
Repeating the computation as in Subsection
2.5
(See also
[
31
, pp. 2608-2609]
)
, we get
O(|w^{0}_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})|)=O(\delta^{2})\varepsilon^{2}
.
Therefore, we get for any
t,s=1,\cdots,N
with
t\neq s

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}}\big)^{m_{i}^{*}-2}=\big(\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{s}}\big)^{m_{i}^{*}-2}\Big(1+O(\delta^{2})\varepsilon^{2}\Big)

for suitable small
\theta>0
.
By a standard approach as in
[
20
, Theorem 2.6]
, we find that

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}}\big)^{m_{i}^{*}-2}=\frac{e^{(m_{i}^{*}-2)H_{i,t}(p_{\varepsilon})}}{e^{(m_{i}^{*}-2)H_{i,s}(p_{\varepsilon})}}+O(\delta^{4-m^{*}})(\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t})^{m^{*}_{i}-2}.

(3.24)

For the case
m^{*}=4
, a similar argument implies that

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}}\big)^{2}=\frac{e^{2H_{i,t}(p_{\varepsilon})}}{e^{2H_{i,s}(p_{\varepsilon})}}+O(\delta^{2})(\varepsilon^{\prime}_{t})^{2}.

(3.25)

This is sufficient to verify Hypothesis (

H
3

).

Now we verify (

⁢
S

E
1

), (

⁢
S

E
2

) and (

⁢
S

E
3

). To do this, we only need to check that

\displaystyle|p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{t,max}|=O(\varepsilon^{2}).

(3.26)

Here,
p_{t,max}
attends

\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{x\in B_{5\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}\big(U_{i,\varepsilon}(x)+w_{i,\varepsilon(x)}\big)
.
Indeed, this follows Assertion (
W_{3}^{I}
) of Proposition
2.15
and a similar argument as
[
31
, Lemma 4.1]
. Consequently,
(
3.22
), (
3.23
), (
3.24
) and (
3.25
) imply (

⁢
S

E
1

), (

⁢
S

E
2

) and (

⁢
S

E
3

).
The proof is completed.

\Box

Appendix A
The Invertibility of a Linear Operator

In this part, we prove the invertibility of the operator
Q_{\varepsilon}L_{\varepsilon}
. A standard method can be found in
[
28
,
22
]
and the references therein. Recall that in (
A
) and (
2.14
) we define

\displaystyle Q_{\varepsilon}(u)=u

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\Big(\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{1}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\sum_{s=1}^{N}e^{v_{1}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}},\cdots,

\displaystyle\quad\quad\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{n}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\sum_{s=1}^{N}e^{v_{n}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}\Big(Z_{3,t,1,\varepsilon}^{*}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{1}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}},\cdots,Z_{3,t,n,\varepsilon}^{*}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{n}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big(s_{1}\sum_{t=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{1}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}},\cdots,s_{n}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{t}e^{v_{n}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big)

and

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}(w_{\varepsilon}^{1},\cdots,w_{\varepsilon}^{n})=-\Delta w_{\varepsilon}^{i}-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}+\frac{K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}.

Moreover, let

\displaystyle L_{\varepsilon}=(L_{1,\varepsilon},\cdots,L_{n,\varepsilon}).

This appendix aims to prove the following theorem.

Theorem A.1

Under Hypothesis (

H
3

), there exists a positive constant
C
independence of
\varepsilon
such that

\|w\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}}+\|w\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|Q_{\varepsilon}L_{\varepsilon}w\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}.

(A.1)

for any
w\in E_{\varepsilon}
. Furthermore,
Q_{\varepsilon}L_{\varepsilon}
is an isomorphism from
E_{\varepsilon}
to
F_{\varepsilon}
.

Remark A.2

Under Hypothesis (

H
3

),
\varepsilon_{t}
are equivalence to each other for
t=1,\cdots,N
.

For any
w_{\varepsilon}\in E_{\varepsilon}
, there exist constants
r_{j,t}
and
s_{i}
for

j=1,2,3
,
t=1,\cdots,N
and
i=1,\cdots,n
such that

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\phi_{i,\varepsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}+s_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}

(A.2)

for
i=1,\cdots,n
.

We begin by estimating the constants
r_{j,t}
and
s_{i}
for

j=1,2,3
,
t=1,\cdots,N
and
i=1,\cdots,n
.

Lemma A.3

Assume that
m^{*}\leq 4
.
For any
j=1,2
and any
t=1,\cdots,N
, we have

\displaystyle r_{j,t}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|,

(A.3)

For
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
, we get

\displaystyle r_{3,t}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-3})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

(A.4)

and

\displaystyle s_{i}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}).

(A.5)

Proof.

First, we prove (
A.3
). Multiplying by
\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}})
on both sides of (
A.2
), integrating over
\Omega
and summing in
i=1,\cdots,n
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon})\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+r_{j,t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})|^{2}\chi_{t}\Big[\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{\varepsilon_{s}}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big].

(A.6)

By a direct computation, we get

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion1})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon})\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{j,t\varepsilon}^{*})w_{i,\varepsilon}.

Here,

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{j,t,\varepsilon}^{*}=-\Delta\Big[\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{l}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\Big]-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})

and we use the symmetry. Moreover, by a pointwise computation, we get

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{j,t,\varepsilon}^{*}=-\Delta\Big[\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{l}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\Big]-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion1})}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|.

On the other hand, we have

RHS of (
A.6
)

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+r_{j,t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})|^{2}\chi_{t}\Big[\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big].

Here,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\chi_{t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})

\displaystyle=O\Big(\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}}}}|h_{i,\varepsilon}(p_{t,\varepsilon}+\varepsilon_{t}y)|\cdot|\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(y)|dy\Big)=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})|^{2}\chi_{t}^{3}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{x_{1}}v_{i}|^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mbox{RHS of (\ref{e:Expansion1})}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{x_{1}}v_{i}|^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big)r_{j,t}.

Plugging these into (
A.6
), we get

\displaystyle r_{j,t}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|

for any
j=1,2
and any
t=1,\cdots,N
.
This proves (
A.3
).

Now we prove (
A.4
) and (
A.5
).
Multiplying by
Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}
on both sides of (
A.2
), integrating over
\Omega
and summing in
i=1,\cdots,n
, we
get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon})Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}+r_{3,t}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}(Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*})^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{l}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{l}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{s}Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}dx+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}Z_{3,i,s,\varepsilon}^{*}dx\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx+\sum_{s\neq t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx\Big].

(A.7)

Here,

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion2})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon})Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}dx=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}(L_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{3,t,\varepsilon}^{*})w_{i,\varepsilon}dx

Notice that

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{3,t,\varepsilon}^{*}

\displaystyle=-\Delta\Big[\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}\Big]-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}+\frac{K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}dx=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-1}).

This implies that

\displaystyle|\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion2})}|=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-1})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|

On the other hand, we get

RHS of (
A.7
)

\displaystyle=\Bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\Bigg\}+\Bigg\{r_{3,t}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}(Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*})^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{l}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{l}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{s}Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}dx+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}Z_{3,i,s,\varepsilon}^{*}dx\Big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\Bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx+\sum_{s\neq t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx\Big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=:A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}.

(A.8)

Here,

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\leq C\varepsilon\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon_{t}^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{N}\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})}}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|dx+C\varepsilon\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\int_{\Omega\backslash\cup_{t=1}^{N}B_{2\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon\|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

(A.9)

and

\displaystyle A_{2}=

\displaystyle r_{3,t}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}(Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*})^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx+\sum_{s\neq t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{l}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{l}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}dx+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\varepsilon_{s}Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}Z_{3,i,s,\varepsilon}^{*}dx\Big].

Notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}(Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*})^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}dx=\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]\varepsilon_{t}^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}(2-\frac{m_{i}^{*}}{2\pi})^{2}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx=\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]\varepsilon_{t}^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{l}(2-\frac{m_{i}^{*}}{2\pi})^{2}\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{l}}}dx=\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]\varepsilon_{t}\varepsilon_{s}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{s}Z_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}^{*}dx

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{s}Z_{3,i,s,\varepsilon}^{*}dx

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}}).

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle=r_{3,t}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{2}+\sum_{s\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m^{*}_{i})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{2}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big].

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle A_{3}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon\Big].

Combining them together, we get

RHS of (
A.7
)

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon\|h_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

\displaystyle\quad+r_{3,t}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{2}+\sum_{s\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m^{*}_{i})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{2}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon\Big].

Plugging this into (
A.7
), we get for any
t=1,\cdots,N

\displaystyle r_{3,t}

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{t}^{2}+\sum_{s\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m^{*}_{i})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\neq t}r_{3,s}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)\varepsilon_{s}\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\varepsilon_{t}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon\Big]=O(\varepsilon\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-1})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|.

In other words, we get for any
t=1,\cdots,N

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{t}r_{3,t}

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)+\sum_{s\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m^{*}_{i})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\neq t}\varepsilon_{s}r_{3,s}\Big[\sum_{l\neq s,l\neq t}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2-m_{i}^{*})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx\Big)+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big[(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|.

(A.10)

Denote

e_{i}=(0,\cdots,0,1,0,\cdots,0)
.
Here,
e_{i}
is a
n
-dimensional vector with
i
-th element equals to
1
and the rest are
0
. Denote
e_{i,j}
by the
j
-th element of
e_{i}
. Integrating (
A.2
) over
\Omega
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}dx+\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}\int_{\Omega}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx+s_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx.

(A.11)

Here,

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion3})}=\int_{\Omega}L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon}dx=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\Omega}(L_{j,\varepsilon}e_{i})w_{j,\varepsilon}dx.

Notice that

L_{j,\varepsilon}e_{i}=0

for
j=1,\cdots,n
and
j\neq i
. And

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}e_{i}=-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)+\frac{\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)dx}{\rho_{i}^{*}}h_{i,\varepsilon}(x).

This implies that

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of (\ref{e:Expansion3})}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{x\in B}|w_{i,\varepsilon}|.

On the other hand, by a direct computation, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\phi_{i,\varepsilon}dx=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}),

(A.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\Big[\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big]dx=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{t}}}\chi_{t}dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\neq t}(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-1})+(2-m_{i}^{*})\varepsilon_{t}(N-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx,

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N}\int_{\Omega}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}dx=N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}).

Here, the first inequality is obtained in a similar way as in (
A.9
).
Plugging these into (
A.11
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}\Big[\varepsilon_{t}(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon\Big]+s_{i}\Big(N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}).

In other words,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{N}(\varepsilon_{t}r_{3,t})\Big[(N-1)(2-m_{i}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big]+s_{i}\Big(N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

(A.13)

for
i=1,\cdots,n
.

Rewriting (
A.10
) and (
A.13
) into matrix form, we denote

\displaystyle X=(\varepsilon_{1}r_{3,1},\cdots,\varepsilon_{N}r_{3N},s_{1},\cdots,s_{n})^{T}

and

\displaystyle\xi=(1,\cdots,1)^{T},

a
(N+n)
-dimensional vector. Define

G_{1}
to be the
N\times N
martix such that

\displaystyle(i,i)\mbox{-element of }G_{1}=(N-1)\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{i}^{*}-2)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}(\nabla v_{i}\cdot x+2)^{2}dx

and

\displaystyle(i,j)\mbox{-element of }G_{1}=(N-2)\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{i}^{*}-2)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}dx.

for
i\neq j
.
Define the
n\times N
matrix

G_{3}=\begin{pmatrix}(N-1)(2-m_{1}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{1}}&\cdots&(N-1)(2-m_{1}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{1}}\\
\vdots&\cdots&\vdots\\
(N-1)(2-m_{n}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{n}}&\cdots&(N-1)(2-m_{n}^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{n}}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Define the
n\times n
diagonal matrix

\displaystyle G_{2}=\mbox{diag}\Big(N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{1}},\cdots,N\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{n}}\Big),

which is invertible.
Then, (
A.10
) and (
A.13
) together give us

\begin{pmatrix}G_{1}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)&G_{3}^{T}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\\
G_{3}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)&G_{2}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\end{pmatrix}X=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})\xi.

By a similar computation as in (
3.1
), the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}G_{1}&G_{3}^{T}\\
G_{3}&G_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

is invertible.
Then, for
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
, we get

\displaystyle r_{3,t}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-3})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})

and

\displaystyle s_{i}=O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\sup|w_{\varepsilon}|+O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}).

This proves (
A.4
) and (
A.5
).

\Box

Before proving Theorem
A.1
, let us recall Theorem
2.1
and Corollary
2.2
.

Theorem A.4

Suppose
\phi=(\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{n})
satisfies

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}-\Delta(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\phi_{l})=e^{v_{i}(y)}\phi_{i}\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
|\phi_{i}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{\tau}\mbox{ for }i=1,\cdots,n\end{array}\right.

for small
\tau>0
. Then,
\phi
is a linear combination of

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{j}}v_{1},\cdots,\partial_{x_{j}}v_{n})\mbox{ for }j=1,2

and

\displaystyle(|x|\dot{v}_{1}(|x|)+2,\cdots,|x|\dot{v}_{n}(|x|)+2).

Corollary A.5

Under the assumptions in the previous theorem, if

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}\phi_{i}dx=0\mbox{ for }j=1,2

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}e^{v_{i}}(|x|\dot{v}_{i}(|x|)+2)\phi_{i}dx=0,

then
\phi_{i}\equiv 0
for
i=1,\cdots,n
.

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

We prove Theorem
A.1
by contradiction. To this end, we assume there exists a sequence of positive numbers
\varepsilon^{(m)}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, two sequence of functions
w_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\in E_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}
and
\phi_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\in F_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\varepsilon^{(m)}\to 0+,\\
Q_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}L_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}w_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}=\phi_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}:=(\phi_{1,\varepsilon^{(m)}},\cdots,\phi_{n,\varepsilon^{(m)}}),\\
\|w_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}}+\|w_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\|_{(L^{\infty}(\Omega))^{n}}=1,\\
\|\phi_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon^{(m)}}}=o\Big(\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(m)}}\Big)\end{array}\right.

(A.14)

as
m\to\infty
. For convenience, throughout the rest this proof, we suppress the
m
in
\varepsilon^{(m)}
and only write
\varepsilon
. We begin by rescaling (
A.2
). For any
t,s=1,\cdots,N
, any
i=1,\cdots,n
and any
j=1,2,3
, we denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=w_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}^{i}_{t,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}w_{j,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{t,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{w}_{t,1,\varepsilon}(y),\cdots,\widetilde{w}_{t,n,\varepsilon}(y)),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=\phi_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=K_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\chi}_{t,s}(y)

\displaystyle:=\chi_{s}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,\varepsilon}^{*}

\displaystyle:=Z^{*}_{j,s,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\{y|\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon}\in\Omega\}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\widetilde{w}_{t,\varepsilon}:=-\Delta_{y}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\widetilde{w}_{t,l,\varepsilon}\Big)-\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}+\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)dy.

Based on these notations, we can rewrite
(
A.2
) into

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\widetilde{w}_{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}+s_{i}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}.

(A.15)

The rest of this proof is proceeded in the following steps.

Step 1. We show that for any
R>0
,
\max_{i=1,\cdots,n,t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{x\in B_{R}(0)}\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(x)\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

By (
A.14
), we know that
|\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}|=|w_{t,i,\varepsilon}|\leq 1
for any
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
.
On the other hand, by Lemma
A.14
and (
A.14
), for
p\in(1,2)
and any
R>0
, we get

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}_{t}\|\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R}(0))}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}_{t}\Big(\int_{B_{R}}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}|^{p}\rho^{p}\frac{dy}{\rho^{p}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\Big(\varepsilon^{4}_{t}\int_{B_{R}(0)}|\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}|^{2}\rho^{2}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{dy}{\rho^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}}\Big)^{\frac{2-p}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}=o(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}).

For any
j=1,2
and any
t=1,\cdots,N
,

\displaystyle\|r_{j,t}\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{P}(B_{R}(0))}

\displaystyle\quad\leq Cr_{j,t}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\cdot\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|=o(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}),

\displaystyle\|r_{3,t}\widetilde{Z}_{t,3,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{P}(B_{R}(0))}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\varepsilon r_{3,t}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|=o(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}})

and

\displaystyle\|s_{i}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R}(0))}=O(s_{i})

\displaystyle=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+O(\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\cdot\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|=o(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}).

By the classical elliptic regularity (see
[
18
]
), there exists bounded continuous functions
\widetilde{w}_{t,i}
for
t=1,\cdots,N
and
i=1,\cdots,n
and a positive constant
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{w}_{t,i}\mbox{ in }C^{\alpha}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Moreover,
\widetilde{w}_{t,i}
satisfies

\displaystyle-\Delta\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\widetilde{w}_{t,l}\Big)=e^{v_{i}(y)}\widetilde{w}_{t,i}+\frac{e^{v_{i}(y)}}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\Bigg[\frac{e^{m_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}{G^{*}}(p_{t}^{*},p_{l}^{*})}h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}{e^{m_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}{G^{*}}(p_{s}^{*},p_{l}^{*})}h_{i}(p_{s}^{*})}\Bigg]^{\frac{2}{m_{i}^{*}-2}}e^{v_{i}(x)}\widetilde{w}_{s,i}dx.

for
i=1,\cdots,n
and
t=1,\cdots,N
.
Recalling that
w_{\varepsilon}\in E_{\varepsilon}
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\Bigg[\frac{e^{m_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}{G^{*}}(p_{t}^{*},p_{l}^{*})}h_{i}(p_{t}^{*})}{e^{m_{1}\sum_{l=1}^{N}{G^{*}}(p_{s}^{*},p_{l}^{*})}h_{i}(p_{s}^{*})}\Bigg]^{\frac{2}{m_{i}^{*}-2}}e^{v_{i}(x)}\widetilde{w}_{s,i}dx=0

for any
i=1,\cdots,n
.
Then,
\widetilde{w}_{t,i}\equiv 0
for any
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
due to Corollary
2.2
. Then,

\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\to 0
in
C_{loc}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. The result follows immediately.

Step 2. For any
\theta>0
, we show that there exists a small constant
\beta_{\theta}>0
such that
\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\|\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\frac{\beta_{\theta}}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(0))}\leq\theta
.

We argue by contradiction. Suppose
there exists a constant
\theta_{0}>0
, a sequence of
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}
and
\beta_{\varepsilon}\to 0
such that

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\|\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\frac{\beta_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}}(0))}\geq\theta_{0}.

(A.16)

Let
x_{\varepsilon}\in B_{\frac{\beta_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}}(0)
be the point where
\widetilde{w}_{t_{0},i}
achieve
\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\|\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\frac{\beta_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}}(0))}
for some
t_{0}=1,\cdots,N
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
t_{0}=1
. This implies that

\displaystyle|x_{\varepsilon}|\leq\frac{\beta_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}=o_{\varepsilon}(1).

(A.17)

By
Step 1
, we know that

|x_{\varepsilon}|\to\infty
.
We define the rescaled functions as follows. For
t=1,\cdots,N
and
i=1,\cdots,n
,

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y):

\displaystyle=\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y),

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{t,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=(\overline{w}_{t,1,\varepsilon}(y),\cdots,\overline{w}_{t,n,\varepsilon}(y)),

\displaystyle\overline{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y),

\displaystyle\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y),

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}_{t,s}(y)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\chi}_{t,s}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y),

\displaystyle\overline{Z}_{t,j,s,\varepsilon}^{*}(y)

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,\varepsilon}^{*}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y)

and

\displaystyle\overline{L}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\overline{w}_{t,\varepsilon}:=-\Delta_{y}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{il}\overline{w}_{t,l,\varepsilon}\Big)-|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}+\frac{|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\frac{1}{|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)dy.

Then, we get

\displaystyle\overline{L}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\overline{w}_{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\overline{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+s_{i}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\overline{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}.

(A.18)

For any
R_{2}>R_{2}>0
, as
\varepsilon\to 0+
, we notice that

\displaystyle|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}=O(|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}})\mbox{ in }L^{\infty}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))\mbox{ sense}

and

\displaystyle\frac{|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\frac{1}{|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}}\overline{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)dy=O(|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}})\mbox{ in }L^{\infty}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))\mbox{ sense.}

For
p\in(1,2)
, we get

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big(\int_{B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0)}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}|x_{\varepsilon}|y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{p}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Big(\int_{\frac{1}{|x_{\varepsilon}|}\cdot B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0)}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}\rho(y)^{2}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\times

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Big(\int_{\frac{1}{|x_{\varepsilon}|}\cdot B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0)}\frac{dy}{\rho(y)^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}}\Big)^{\frac{2-p}{2p}}

By (
A.17
), we get

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\Big(\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}\rho(y)^{2}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\times

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\Big(\int_{\frac{1}{|x_{\varepsilon}|}\cdot B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0)}\frac{dy}{\rho(y)^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}}\Big)^{\frac{2-p}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq C|x_{\varepsilon}|^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\|\phi_{i}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}=o_{\varepsilon}(1).

Moreover, for
j=1,2
we get

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\overline{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{1-m^{*}}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|)|x_{\varepsilon}|^{1-m^{*}}=o_{\varepsilon}(1),

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\overline{Z}_{t,3,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\overline{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{3,t}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|)|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}}=o_{\varepsilon}(1)

and

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N}s_{i}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\overline{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(|x_{\varepsilon}|y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|_{L^{p}(B_{R_{2}}(0)\backslash B_{R_{1}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{s=1}^{N}s_{1}|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}}=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|)|x_{\varepsilon}|^{2-m^{*}}=o_{\varepsilon}(1).

Then, there exists a bounded harmonic function
\overline{w}_{t,i,\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\to\overline{w}_{t,i,\infty}\mbox{ in }L_{loc}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}).

Moreover, by Liouville theorem,
\overline{w}_{t,i,\infty}
is a constant. Denote it by
c_{t,i}
.
By (
A.16
), we get

\displaystyle\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}c_{t,i}\geq\theta_{0}>0.

(A.19)

In other words, for any
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(x)=c_{t,i}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\mbox{ for }R_{1}|x_{\varepsilon}|\leq|y|\leq R_{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|.

(A.20)

Define

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(s)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(s(\cos\theta,\sin\theta))d\theta.

We get

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(s)=c_{t,i}+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\mbox{ for }R_{1}|x_{\varepsilon}|\leq s\leq R_{2}|x_{\varepsilon}|.

immediately.

On the other hand, (
A.15
) implies that

\displaystyle-\Delta

\displaystyle(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{ij}\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon})-e^{v_{i}(y)}\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}+\frac{e^{v_{i}(y)}}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widetilde{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}(s\cos\theta,s\sin\theta)d\theta+\frac{r_{3,t}e^{v_{i}(y)}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\chi_{t}Z_{3,i,\varepsilon}(s\cos\theta,s\sin\theta)+\frac{s_{i}}{2\pi}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(y)}.

Notice that
w_{\varepsilon}\in E_{\varepsilon}
, we get

\int_{\Omega_{t,\varepsilon}}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)dy=0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle-\Delta

\displaystyle(\sum_{l=1}^{n}a^{ij}\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon})-e^{v_{i}(y)}\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widetilde{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}(s\cos\theta,s\sin\theta)d\theta+\frac{r_{3,t}e^{v_{i}(y)}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\chi_{t}Z_{3,i,\varepsilon}(s\cos\theta,s\sin\theta)+\frac{s_{i}}{2\pi}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(y)}.

(A.21)

Multiplying the both sides of (
A.21
) by
s
and integrating from
0
to
t
, we get

\displaystyle-t\frac{d}{dt}

\displaystyle(\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(t))-\int_{B_{t}(0)}e^{v_{i}(y)}\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(|y|)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{t}(0)}\widetilde{\phi}_{i,\varepsilon}dy+r_{3,t}\int_{B_{t}(0)}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(y)}\widetilde{Z}_{3,i,t,\varepsilon}(y)dy+s_{i}\int_{B_{t}(0)}\chi_{t}e^{v_{i}(y)}dy.

In other words,

\displaystyle-t\frac{d}{dt}

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(t)-\int_{B_{t}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}e^{v_{j}(y)}\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(|y|)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{t}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}\widetilde{\phi}_{j,\varepsilon}dy+r_{3,t}\int_{B_{t}(0)}\chi_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}e^{v_{i}(y)}\widetilde{Z}_{3,j,t,\varepsilon}(y)dy+\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}s_{j}\int_{B_{t}(0)}\chi_{t}e^{v_{j}(y)}dy.

(A.22)

In order to proceed with our proof, we need the following estimate.

Claim A.6

For any small
\tau>0
, there exsits a positive constant
C_{\tau}
such that

\max_{t=1,\cdots,N,i=1,\cdots,n}|w_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)|\leq\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)\cdot C_{\tau}(1+|y|)^{\tau}}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}.

We will sketch the proof of Claim
A.6
at the end of this appendix since this is similar to Proposition
2.13
.

By a similar computation as in
Step 1
, we get

RHS of (
A.22
)

\displaystyle=O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+\varepsilon^{-1}O_{m^{*}}\cdot\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|=o_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\Big)+\varepsilon^{-1}O_{m^{*}}.

On the other hand, for
t
sufficiently large, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B_{t}(0)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}e^{v_{j}(y)}\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(|y|)dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\int_{B_{t}(0)}\frac{dy}{(1+|y|)^{m^{*}-\tau}}=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}

for sufficiently small
\tau
.
Then, we get

\frac{d}{dt}\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(t)=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{t}
.
Integrating the both side from
R
to
|x_{\varepsilon}|
, we get

\displaystyle|\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(|x_{\varepsilon}|)|\leq|\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(R)|+\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)\ln\frac{|x_{\varepsilon}|}{R}}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}.

Recall that
|x_{\varepsilon}|=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\varepsilon}
, as we assumed. For sufficiently large
R
and small
\varepsilon
, we get

|\hat{w}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(|x_{\varepsilon}|)|\leq\frac{\theta_{0}}{2}

for any
t=1,\cdots,N
and any
i=1,\cdots,n
. Here,
\theta_{0}
is the positive constant appears in (
A.16
).
This contradicts with (
A.19
) and (
A.20
).

Step 3. Estimating
\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}}
.

Now we come back to (
A.2
), i.e.

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}w_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\phi_{i,\varepsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}+s_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}

for
i=1,\cdots,n
. Here,

\displaystyle L_{i,\varepsilon}(w_{\varepsilon}^{1},\cdots,w_{\varepsilon}^{n})=-\Delta w_{\varepsilon}^{i}-K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}+\frac{K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}.

For the sake of simplicity, we write
B:=\cup_{t=1}^{N}B_{\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})
.
By the classical elliptic estimate (see, for instance,
[
18
]
), we get

\displaystyle\|w^{i}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|w^{i}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial B)}+C\|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{N}r_{3,t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big\|s_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}+C\Big\|\frac{K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)}{\rho_{i}^{*}}\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}.

Here,
\|w^{i}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial B)}
is sufficiently small due to
Step 2
. Denote it by
\theta
. Moreover, we get

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\varepsilon,i}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}\leq C\|\phi_{\varepsilon,i}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}=o_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\Big),

\displaystyle\Big\|r_{j,t}\partial_{x_{j}}v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{t,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{t}})\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}\leq C(\|\phi_{i,\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2})\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{p}-2+m^{*}-2}=o_{\varepsilon}(1),

\displaystyle\Big\|

\displaystyle r_{3,t}Z_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[O(\varepsilon^{-1}\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+\varepsilon^{m^{*}-2}\cdot\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|\Big]\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{p}+m^{*}-3}=o_{\varepsilon}(1),

\displaystyle\Big\|s_{i}

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N}\chi_{s}e^{v_{i}(\frac{x-p_{s,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{s}})+2\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega\backslash B)}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})+\varepsilon^{-1}O_{m^{*}}\cdot\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\max_{t=1,\cdots,N}\sup_{y\in B_{2\delta}(p_{t,\varepsilon_{t}})}|w_{i,\varepsilon}(y)|\Big]\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{p}+m^{*}-4}=o_{\varepsilon}(1)

and

\int_{\Omega}K_{i,\varepsilon}(x)w_{i,\varepsilon}=0

since
w_{\varepsilon}\in E_{\varepsilon}
.
Here, we used Lemma
A.3
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\theta+o_{\varepsilon}(1).

(A.23)

Now we estimate
\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}}
.
By (
A.15
), for any
t=1,\cdots,N
we get

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{y}\widetilde{w}^{i}_{t,\varepsilon}{\widetilde{\rho}_{\beta}}\|_{L^{2}(B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}|{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}}\cdot(1+|y|)^{1+{\frac{\beta}{2}}}|^{2}dy+C\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\phi_{t,j,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\|\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}(0))}

\displaystyle\quad+\|s_{i}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0))}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}|{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\widetilde{K}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\widetilde{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}}\cdot(1+|y|)^{1+{\frac{\beta}{2}}}|^{2}dy

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}\frac{(1+|y|)^{{2+\beta}}}{(1+|y|)^{2m^{*}}}|\widetilde{w}^{i}_{t,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}dy

\displaystyle\leq C{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\delta})}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|\phi_{t,j,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}dy\leq{\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}}

and

\displaystyle\|\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{s=1}^{N}r_{j,t}\widetilde{Z}_{t,j,s,i,\varepsilon}^{*}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}(0))}

\displaystyle\quad+\|s_{i}\sum_{l=1}^{N}\widetilde{\chi}_{t,l}e^{v_{i}(y+\frac{p_{t,\varepsilon}-p_{l,\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon_{l}})+2\ln\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{\varepsilon_{l}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{\frac{2\delta_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(0))}\leq[\varepsilon^{-1}O_{m^{*}}|w_{\varepsilon}|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+O(\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})]^{2}.

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{w}_{t,\varepsilon}^{i}\hat{\rho}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}|\widetilde{w}^{i}_{t,\varepsilon}(\varepsilon_{t}y+p_{t,\varepsilon})|^{2}\cdot\frac{dy}{(1+|y|)^{2}\big(\ln(2+|y|)\big)^{2+\alpha}}\leq C{|w_{t,\varepsilon}^{i}|_{L^{\infty}(B_{2\delta}(0))}^{2}}.

By a standard elliptic estimate (
[
18
]
) on
\Omega\backslash\cup_{t=1}^{N}B_{2\delta_{t}}(p_{t,\varepsilon})
, we get

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{\mathbb{X}_{\varepsilon}}\leq C(\|w_{\varepsilon}^{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\|\phi_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}})\leq C\Big(\theta+o\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\Big).

Combining (
A.23
), we obtain a contradiction with (
A.14
).
Now (
A.1
) is proved.

Step 4.
Q_{\varepsilon}
is an isomorphism.

The result follows from the Fredholm alternative.

\Box

Now we are in the position to prove Claim
A.6
.

Proof of Claim
A.6
.

We only sketch the proof since this is similar to Proposition
2.13
.
To prove this, inspired by
[
31
,
5
]
, we argue by contradiction as follows. Denote

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\varepsilon}:=\max_{t=1,\cdots,n,i=1,\cdots,N}\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)}{(1+|y|)^{\gamma}}.

If
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}=o_{\varepsilon}(1)
, then the proof is completed.
Suppose that there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\geq c_{0}
and
y_{\varepsilon}\in B_{\frac{2\delta_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(0)
attends
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}
. Denote

\displaystyle\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y):=\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}.

An immediate observation is that

\displaystyle|\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)|=\Big|\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\hat{w}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y|)^{\tau}}\cdot\frac{(1+|y|)^{\tau}}{(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big|\leq\frac{(1+|y|)^{\tau}}{(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}.

Then,

\displaystyle-\Delta_{y}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,j,\varepsilon}\Big)-e^{v_{i}(y)}\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,j,\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\hat{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}+\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot r_{3,t}e^{v_{i}(y)}\widetilde{Z}_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}+\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot s_{i}e^{v_{i}(y)}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}.

(A.24)

Here,

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}(s)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widetilde{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}(s\cdot\cos\theta,s\cdot\sin\theta)d\theta.

Our argument is divided into three parts with respect to the growth of
|y_{\varepsilon}|
. For all of the three cases, we will draw contradictions.

Case 1.
|y_{\varepsilon}|
is bounded.

By a similar approach as in
Step 1
of the proof of Theorem
A.1
, we get for any
R>0

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\hat{\phi}_{\varepsilon,i}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(B_{R}(0))}=\Big\|\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot r_{3,t}e^{v_{i}(y)}\widetilde{Z}_{3,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(B_{R}(0))}=\Big\|\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot s_{i}e^{v_{i}(y)}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(B_{R}(0))}=o_{\varepsilon}(1),

This implies that

\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}\to w^{*}_{t,i}\mbox{ in }C_{loc}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

with
\beta\in(0,1)
and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}-\Delta\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{ij}w_{t,j}^{*}\Big)-e^{v_{i}(y)}w_{t,i}^{*}=0\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
|w_{t,i}^{*}(y)|\leq C(1+|y|)^{\tau}\mbox{ and }w_{t,i}^{*}\mbox{ are radial};\\
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{v_{i}(y)}(y\cdot\nabla v_{i}(y)+2)w_{t,i}^{*}(y)dy=0\\
w_{t,i}^{*}(y_{0})=1\mbox{ with }y_{0}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}y_{\varepsilon}.\end{array}\right.

Here, the third assertion is due to the fact that
w_{\varepsilon}\in E_{\varepsilon}
. This contradicts with Corollary
2.2
.

Case 2.
|y_{\varepsilon}|
is unbounded and
|y_{\varepsilon}|=o_{\varepsilon}(1)\varepsilon^{-1}
.

By a similar computation as in
Case 1
, we know that
\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(0)\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. On the other hand, we always have

\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})=\pm 1
.
Then, by Green’s formula, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})-\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,i,\varepsilon}(0)|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\{\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\Big|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)\Big|\cdot\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}e^{v_{j}(\eta)}\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(\eta)\Big|d\eta\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg\{\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\Big|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)\Big|\cdot\Big|\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}\hat{\phi}_{\varepsilon,j}(\eta)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}+\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot r_{3,t}\sum_{j=1}^{n}a_{ij}e^{v_{j}(\eta)}\widetilde{Z}_{3,t,j,\varepsilon}^{*}(\eta)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{n}s_{j}e^{v_{j}(y)}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big|\Bigg\}d\eta=:A+B.

(A.25)

Here,
G_{\varepsilon}(y,\eta)
denotes the Green’s function with Dirichlet boundary condition on
B_{\frac{2\delta_{t}}{\varepsilon_{t}}}(0)
. The boundary terms are canceled out since
\widetilde{w}^{*}_{t,i,\varepsilon}
is radial.
We will draw a contradiction by proving that
A+B=o_{\varepsilon}(1)
.

Notice that we have the pointwise estimate

\displaystyle\Big|e^{v_{j}(\eta)}\hat{\hat{w}}_{t,j,\varepsilon}(\eta)\Big|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}(1+|\eta|)^{m^{*}-\tau}}.

(A.26)

On the other hand, by similar computations as in (
2.43
), we get for
p\in(1,2)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\Bigg|\frac{\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot\hat{\phi}_{\varepsilon,j}(\eta)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Bigg|^{p}d\eta\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}.

(A.27)

Here, we use (
A.14
). As in (
2.44
) and (
2.45
) we get for
p\in(1,2)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\Bigg|\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot r_{j,t}e^{v_{i}(\eta)}\widetilde{Z}_{j,t,i,\varepsilon}^{*}(\eta)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Bigg|^{p}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}

(A.28)

for
j=1,2,3

and

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\Bigg|\frac{\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\cdot s_{i}e^{v_{i}(\eta)}}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Bigg|^{p}d\eta\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}=\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}.

(A.29)

Here, we use (
A.14
) and Lemma
A.3
.
To estimate
A+B
, we apply
[
31
, Theorem 3.2]
(see also
[
5
, Proposition 3.1]
). By
[
31
, Lemma 3.2]
, we get

|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|\leq\left\{\begin{aligned} &C(\ln|y_{\varepsilon}|+|\ln|\eta||)&\mbox{ if }\eta\in\Sigma_{1}:=\{\eta\in B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)||\eta|<\frac{|y_{\varepsilon}|}{2}\};\\
&C(\ln|y_{\varepsilon}|+|\ln|y-\eta||)&\mbox{ if }\eta\in\Sigma_{2}:=\{\eta\in B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)||y_{\varepsilon}-\eta|<\frac{|y_{\varepsilon}|}{2}\};\\
&\frac{C|y_{\varepsilon}|}{|\eta|}&\mbox{ if }\eta\in\Sigma_{3}:=B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}(0)}\backslash(\Sigma_{1}\cup\Sigma_{2}).\end{aligned}\right.

(A.30)

In follows, we estimate
A+B
with the help of (
A.26
), (
A.27
), (
A.28
), (
A.29
) and (
A.30
).

As for
A
, by a similar method as in (
2.52
), (
2.53
), (
2.28
), (
2.54
) and (
2.55
), we get

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}(0)}\frac{|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|d\eta}{(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}(1+|\eta|)^{m^{*}-\tau}}=C\Big\{\int_{\Sigma_{1}}+\int_{\Sigma_{2}}+\int_{\Sigma_{3}}\Big\}\frac{|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|d\eta}{(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}(1+|\eta|)^{m^{*}-\tau}}=O_{\varepsilon}(1).

On the other hand, by a similar method as in (
2.56
), (
2.57
), (
2.58
) and (
2.59
), we get

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big(\int_{B_{\frac{2\delta}{\varepsilon}}}|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|^{p^{\prime}}d\eta\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{o_{\varepsilon}(1)}{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}(1+|y_{\varepsilon}|)^{\tau}}\Big[\Big(\int_{\Sigma_{1}}|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|^{p^{\prime}}d\eta\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}+\Big(\int_{\Sigma_{2}}|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|^{p^{\prime}}d\eta\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\Big(\int_{\Sigma_{3}}|G_{\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon},\eta)-G_{\varepsilon}(0,\eta)|^{p^{\prime}}d\eta\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\Big]=:o_{\varepsilon}(1)

Hence, we get

A+B=o_{\varepsilon}(1)
, which is a contradiction.

Case 3.
|y_{\varepsilon}|
is unbounded and
|y_{\varepsilon}|\simeq\varepsilon^{-1}
.

Combining
[
5
, Proposition 3.1]
,
we obtain a contradiction by a similar approach as in
Case 3
of Proposition
2.13
. Claim

A.6
is proved.

\Box
